L917: A total of 134 patients were in the pooled PK/PD dataset. The pooled population comprised 111 (82.8%) patients with AHP and 23 (17.2%) subjects that are CHE. Patients were predominantly females (87.3%) with median age (range) of 38.0 (19.0, 65.0) years and median body weight
L918: (range) of 66.2 (39.5, 131.3) kg. The median baseline ALA level was approximately 2-fold higher in AHP patients (15.7 mmol/mol Cr) compared to subjects that are CHE (6.80 mmol/mol Cr).
L919: The covariate effects of age, hepatic and renal functions, baseline ALA, patient type (subjects that are CHE versus patients with AHP), and race (East Asian versus non-East Asian) were investigated in population PK/PD models. Since absolute dose (mg) of givosiran received by a patient was based on body weight, the effect of body weight on ALA lowering was investigated by simulations from the final PK/PD model.
L920: There were only 5 non-AIP patients (2 with VP, 2 with HCP, and 1 with other) in Study 003. Two patients were randomized to givosiran treatment group and 3 patients were randomized to placebo group during the DB period. Thus, there were not enough non-AIP patients to evaluate as a covariate in the PK/PD analysis.
L921: The numbers of subjects and urinary ALA samples included in the PK/PD analysis are summarized in Table 43. A total of 2600 measurable urinary ALA samples from 134 subjects in placebo and givosiran treatment groups were available for PK/PD modeling. The Phase 3 Study 003 represented 47.4% of the overall urinary ALA samples included in the population PK/PD. analysis.

L926: ## Base PK/PD Model Development
L927: Development of the population PK/PD model of givosiran on ALA was driven by the mechanistic hypotheses, statistical considerations, and heuristics guided by observed trends in the data. A schematic of the population PK/PD model of ALA is shown in Figure 26.
L928: The effect of allometrically scaled concentrations of active siRNA in liver on urinary ALA levels was modeled as an inhibitory effect on the synthesis rate of ALA through an intermediary RISC effect compartment.
L929: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212194}
## {GIVLAARI (givosiran)}
L930: In AHP patients, an additive effect of hemin and givosiran was assumed and included in the model as an additional inhibitory effect on the synthesis rate of ALA. The elimination rate constant of ALA was fixed to 0.84 \text{ hr}^{-1} (half-life=0.825 \text{ hours}) from literature.[Floderus 2006]
L931: Active siRNA turnover rate from the peripheral liver compartment to the RISC compartment (Q2) was estimated to account for the equilibrium delay between the liver and RISC compartments, which translated into a delay in PD effect relative to the projected liver concentrations.
L932: Exploratory analysis indicated that baseline ALA levels in subjects that are CHE were approximately 2-fold higher than in AHP patients, and the duration of PD effect was longer in subjects that are CHE. Thus, different formation rate constant of ALA (K_{in,ALA}) and different IC_{50,givo} were estimated for patients with AHP and subjects that are CHE.
L933: Figure 26. Schematic Representation of Population PK/PD Model for ALA in Humans
L934: Abbreviations: ALA=urinary aminolevulinic acid compartment; C_{eff,hemin}=hemin concentration of effect compartment; CL=clearance; CL_{RISC}=clearance from RNA-induced silencing complex (RISC) loaded drug; IC_{50}=concentration required to produce half-maximal effect of givosiran; I_{max}=maximum inhibitory effect of givosiran on K_{in,ALA}; k_a=uptake rate constant to liver; k_{0,hemin}=first-order rate constant of hemin from plasma to effect compartment; k_{hemin}=elimination rate constant for hemin; K_{in,ALA}=zero-order synthesis rate of ALA; K_{out,ALA}=first-order degradation rate constant for ALA; Q=intercompartmental clearance; Q2=turnover rate of givosiran from peripheral liver compartment into RISC; V1=volume of distribution of central liver compartment; V2=volume of distribution of peripheral liver compartment.
L935: (Source: Applicant's PK/PD Report, Figure 12)
L936: Differential equations used for the population PK/PD model (indirect response model) of urinary ALA are presented below.
L937: \frac{dALA}{dt} = K_{in,ALA} \times \text{GivosiranEffect} \times \text{HeminEffect} - K_{out,ALA} \times ALA
L938: where K_{in,ALA} is the zero-order formation rate of urinary ALA, and K_{out,ALA} is the first-order degradation rate of urinary ALA. As part of the above model, "GivosiranEffect" is the effect of
L939: active siRNA concentrations in RISC on Kin,ALA. Drug effect of givosiran was modeled using inhibitory effect model (I_{max}) as presented below.
L940: GivosiranEffect = \left( 1 - I_{max,givo} \cdot \frac{C_{RISC}(t)}{IC_{50,givo} + C_{RISC}(t)} \right)
L941: where C_{RISC}(t) is the RISC concentration of active siRNA at time 't' predicted with a PK model. I_{max,givo} represents maximum inhibition effect of active siRNA on Kin,ALA and IC_{50,givo} represents the RISC concentration of active siRNA reaching 50% of maximum inhibition of givosiran.
L942: For the modeling of hemin effect, hemin plasma PK was not collected in the studies and only dosing information was present in the dataset. Therefore, hemin plasma concentrations were predicted using a 1-compartment model using the literature-reported value for the elimination rate constant 'k_e' of 0.0642 hr^{-1} (half-life is approximately 10.8 hours).[Tokola 1986] However, this could not describe the duration of hemin effect on ALA and resulted in systematic underestimation of duration of hemin effect on urinary ALA. Therefore, effect compartment model was used to explain the time delay between plasma concentration of hemin and the PD response. The inhibitory effect of hemin on K_{in,ALA} was tested using I_{max} and linear function models. A linear inhibitory model was selected since the model-estimated maximum inhibition of hemin (64.2%) was underestimated by the I_{max} model. "HeminEffect" is the hemin inhibitory effect on K_{in,ALA}. Drug effect of hemin was modeled using linear inhibitory model as presented below.
L943: HeminEffect = \left( 1 - Slope \cdot C_{eff,hemin}(t) \right)
L944: where C_{eff,hemin}(t) is the effect compartment concentration of hemin at time 't' predicted with a reference value.
L945: ## Final Population PK/PD Model
L946: The covariate analysis indicated that age, body weight, renal impairment, sex, and race were not significant and therefore were not included the final PK/PD model. Four covariates were retained in the final PK/PD model: patient type (subjects that are CHE versus patients with AHP) on IC_{50,givo} and K_{in,ALA}, mild hepatic impairment on K_{in,ALA}, and baseline ALA on I_{max,givo}. The parameter estimates derived with the final PK/PD model are presented in Table 44.

L960: ## Effect of Body Weight on Givosiran PD
L961: The impact of body weight was incorporated in the PK/PD model using allometric exponents. All clearance parameters were scaled by an exponent of 0.75 and all volume parameters were scaled by an exponent of 1.
L962: Covariate evaluation showed that the impact of body weight on PD parameters (I_{max} and IC_{50}) was not statistically significant. Since the dose of givosiran is based on body weight, patients with lower body weight received a lower absolute dose compared to patients with higher body weight. The impact of body weight on steady state urinary ALA levels was investigated using simulations from the final PK/PD model.
L963: Simulations showed that ALA levels and % reduction in ALA at steady state were similar across the range of body weights observed in clinical studies. PD response in patients weighing 40 kg
L964: (lowest body weight across studies) and 130 kg (highest body weight across studies) were comparable to that in patients weighing 66.2 kg (median body weight across studies; see Figure 35).
L965: **Figure 28. Model-Predicted Absolute and Percent Change from Baseline ALA in AHP Patients Weighting 40, 66.2 and 130 kg After 2.5 mg/kg Once Monthly Dose of Givosiran**
L966: **Abbreviations:** AHP=acute hepatic porphyria; ALA=aminolevulinic acid; PI=prediction interval.
(Source: Applicant's PK/PD Report, Figure 19)

L972: ## Reviewer's Comments on the E-R Analysis for Urine ALA:
L973: *The applicant's analysis establishes distribution to liver based in part on rat distribution to liver data and allometric scaling principles. While this may be a reasonable approach to estimate the*
L974: *liver concentrations, some degree of uncertainty remains as to the accuracy of this model for human PK in the liver, since this cannot be measured directly.*
L975: *The analysis does appear to capture the central tendency of the data, which makes this reasonable for descriptive purposes.*
L976: (b) (4)
L977: ## E-R Analysis for Safety: Serum Creatinine Concentrations and Liver ALT Levels
L978: ## Methods
L979: Longitudinal serum ALT and SCr measurements obtained following givosiran administration from long-term multiple dose studies in AHP patients (Study 002 and Study 003) were pooled for the analysis.
L980: The Safety Population data included 64 AHP patients who received givosiran 1.25 mg/kg, 2.5 mg/kg, and 5.0 mg/kg once monthly, and 5.0 mg/kg once quarterly.
L981: A summary of relevant safety variables analyzed in this section is presented in Table 46.

L987: ## Analysis of ALT Elevations in Studies 002/003
L988: In Study 003, 6/48 patients treated with 2.5 mg/kg once monthly givosiran and 1/46 patients treated with placebo had AEs mapping to the Drug-Related Hepatic Disorders Standardized Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) Query (SMQ). All 7 patients had ALT elevations &gt; 3x ULN, which was the protocol-defined threshold for enhanced monitoring with hepatic assessments. Additionally, another patient (USUBJID=ALN-AS1-003-(b) (6)) had ALT&gt;3x ULN that was not considered as an AE, but was included in the population safety analysis. Therefore, the analysis of correlations between givosiran PK and/or PD and ALT elevations was conducted by categorizing the patients by whether they had any postdose measurement of ALT &gt;3x ULN in Studies 002 and 003.
L989: In Study 003, a total of 7 patients treated with givosiran had ALT elevations &gt;3x ULN. No patient in Study 002 had ALT elevations &gt;3x ULN.
L990: ## Relationship between ALT Elevations and Givosiran Plasma PK
L991: The applicant concluded that there is no correlation between the maximum postdose ALT levels and plasma Cmax for givosiran or its metabolite (Figure 30).
L992: Figure 30. Study 002/003 – Lack of Relationship Between ALT Elevations and Plasma Cmax for Givosiran and AS(N-1)3' Givosiran
L993: Abbreviations: ALT=alanine aminotransferase; C_{max}= maximum observed plasma concentration; ULN=upper limit of normal.
L994: Note: C_{max} is from Month 6 in Study 002 and from Month 5 in Study 003. All measurable concentrations at Month 6 for Study 002 and Month 5 for Study 003 are shown. AS(N-1)3' givosiran was not measured in Study 002. A total of 4/7 patients with ALT&gt;3xULN had measurable givosiran concentrations, 2 were below the limit of quantitation, and 1 patient had a missing PK sample. A total of 53/57 patients with ALT\le3xULN had measurable givosiran concentrations, 3 had missing PK samples, and 1 patient discontinued.
L995: (Source: Applicant's Population PK/PD Report, Figure 26)
L996: ## Relationship between ALT Elevations and Givosiran Predicted Liver PK
L997: Based on Figure 31 and Figure 32 the applicant concluded:
L998: Model-predicted maximum liver Cmax of givosiran in patients who had ALT &gt;3x ULN were comparable to patients without ALT elevations for patients receiving givosiran 2.5 mg/kg monthly indicating that ALT elevations were not correlated with predicted target site exposure of givosiran (Figure 31). A comparison of model-predicted liver concentrations across all dose regimens in Studies 002 and 003 showed a higher liver Cmax for 5 mg/kg monthly and 5 mg/kg quarterly relative to other doses, however, there were no ALT elevations from Study 002 (Figure 32). This indicates that there were no ALT elevations in patients at liver Cmax of approximately 2- to 3-fold higher than the average liver Cmax in patients with ALT &gt;3x ULN following the therapeutic dose of 2.5 mg/kg monthly.

L1012: ## **Reviewer's Comments for the Applicant's E-R for Safety Analysis:**
L1013: The applicant's E-R analysis for ALT elevation is certainly limited by the small number of ALT events &gt;3\times ULN (n=7). This number makes it hard to evaluate rates of safety across different concentrations. Instead the applicant has chosen to evaluate concentration as a function of event. The most assuring piece of information comes from Figure 32 which highlights that subjects with exposure 2-3 fold higher than the 2.5 mg/kg dose, who received the 5 mg/kg dose did not experience these ALT elevations.
L1014: ## **19.4.6 Immunogenicity**
L1015: Immunogenicity was assessed in all 4 studies. For the registration Phase 3 trial Study ENVISION, ADA assessment were conducted at baseline, Day 29, Months 3, 6, 7, 12, 18, 24, 36. A validated enzyme linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) with a LLOQ of 37.4 ng/mL and minimal required dilution (MRD) of 50-fold was used to assess the immunogenicity potential of givosiran by detecting serum immunoglobulin (Ig) G (IgG)/IgM antibodies against givosiran.
L1016: In the DB period, 1/46 (2.2%) patient in the placebo group and 2/48 (4.2%) patients in the givosiran group tested positive for ADA at baseline. The ADA titer in these 3 patients increased by less than 4-fold (two 2x serial dilutions) during the study (titer \le100). In the combined DB
L1017: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212194}
## {GIVLAARI (givosiran)}
L1018: and OLE period, 1/94 (1.06%) patients treated with givosiran developed de novo ADA during the study.
L1019: Presence of ADA appears to have no impact on the PK of givosiran. In the 2 patients randomized to givosiran in the DB period who were ADA-positive at baseline, and the 1 patient with treatment-induced ADA in the OLE period, concentrations of givosiran and AS(N-1)3' givosiran were comparable to those in ADA-negative patients (Figure 34).
L1020: **Figure 34. Comparison of Givosiran and AS(N-1)3' Givosiran Concentrations in ADA-Positive and ADA-Negative Patients**
L1021: Similarly, presence of ADA appears to have no impact on the PD of givosiran (Figure 35).
L1022: **Figure 35. Comparison of Change from Baseline ALA Levels Between ADA-Positive and ADA-Negative Patients**

The left plot shows ALA/PBG Level on Day 42 (mmol/mol Cr) versus DOSE (mg/kg). The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 0.1 to 10.0. L1055: Flowchart illustrating the Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Program.
The program is divided into two main categories:
Clinical Pharmacology Studies (Phase 1): This category includes studies conducted in Chronic High Excreters (CHE) subjects.
Efficacy and Safety Dataa (Phase 1, 2, and 3): This category includes studies conducted in Acute Hepatic Porphyria (AHP) patients.
Both categories feed into the study phases:
Phase 1/2 (ALN-AS1-001):Study 001A/B: Part A SAD; Part B MAD (completed) N=23
Study 001Ca,b: Part C Multiple Dose (3 months dosing; completed) N=17

Phase 3 (ALN-AS1-003 (ENVISION)):Study 003 DB period (6 months dosing; completed) N=94
Study 003 OLE Period (up to 29 months dosing; ongoing) N=93

Study 004 (DDI Study Single Dose (completed) N=10) is also shown, connected to the Phase 1/2 studies.

L1061: Figure 22 displays six plots assessing the goodness-of-fit of the final Population PK Model for Givosiran. The plots are arranged in two rows of three columns each.
The top row shows Observed Concentration (ng/mL) versus Population Predicted Concentration (ng/mL) (left), Observed Concentration (ng/mL) versus Individual Predicted Concentration (ng/mL) (middle), and Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Population Predicted Concentration (ng/mL) (right).
The bottom row shows Observed Concentration (ng/mL) versus Population Predicted Concentration (ng/mL) (left), Observed Concentration (ng/mL) versus Individual Predicted Concentration (ng/mL) (middle), and Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Time After Last Dose (h) (right).
In all concentration plots, the x-axis and y-axis are logarithmic. The plots show observed data points (black dots) and a LOESS locally weighted scatterplot smoothing line (blue line). The identity line (IDENT, black line) is also shown. A horizontal dashed red line indicates the Lower Limit of Quantitation (LLOQ).
The conditional weighted residual plots show residuals centered around zero, with the LOESS line indicating a slight trend.

L1063: Figure 25 illustrates the schematic representation of the Givosiran PK/PD Model, showing the transition from Nonclinical PKPD Modeling (left) to Clinical PKPD Modeling (right) via Allometric Scaling.
Nonclinical PKPD Modeling (Left): The model shows the following components and relationships:
Dose leads to Liver PK.
Liver PK leads to RISC PK.
RISC PK has a positive (+) influence on ALAS1 mRNA.
ALAS1 mRNA is subject to Synthesis Rate and Degradation Rate.
Clinical PKPD Modeling (Right): The model shows the following components and relationships:
Dose leads to Liver PK.
Liver PK leads to RISC PK.
RISC PK has a negative (-) influence on Urinary ALA.
Urinary ALA is subject to Synthesis Rate and Degradation Rate.
The transition from Nonclinical to Clinical modeling is indicated by an arrow labeled "Allometric Scaling".
L1064: Diagram illustrating the Population PK/PD Model for ALA in Humans. The model consists of several compartments and processes:
Compartments: Central Liver (V1), Peripheral Liver (V2), RISC (V1 + V2), ALA, Hemin (Plasma), and Hemin (Effect Comp).
Processes:Uptake to Central Liver: k_a
Intercompartmental Clearance (Central to Peripheral): Q
Clearance from Peripheral Liver: CL
Turnover from Peripheral Liver to RISC: Q2
Clearance from RISC: CL_{RISC}
ALA Synthesis: K_{in,ALA} \times \text{Drug Effect}
ALA Degradation: K_{out,ALA}
Hemin Clearance (Plasma to Effect Comp): k_{0,hemin}
Hemin Elimination: k_{hemin}

Drug Effect: The Drug Effect is modulated by Inhibitory Givosiran Effect and Inhibitory Hemin Effect.
Inhibitory Givosiran Effect: 1 - \frac{I_{max} \times C_{RISC}}{IC_{50} + C_{RISC}}
Inhibitory Hemin Effect: 1 - \text{Slope} \times C_{eff,hemin}
L1065: Figure 27 displays four goodness-of-fit plots for the Final Population PK/PD Model. The top row shows scatter plots of Ln(Observed urine ALA) versus Ln(Population Predicted urine ALA) and Ln(Observed urine ALA) versus Ln(Individual Predicted urine ALA). The bottom row shows scatter plots of Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Ln(Population Predicted urine ALA) and Conditional Weighted Residuals versus Time (days). In all plots, the black dots represent the data points, the gray line represents the line of identity (IDENT), and the blue line represents the LOESS smoothing line.

L1067: Figure 30 displays two box plots comparing Plasma Cmax (ng/mL) for Givosiran and AS(N-1)3' Givosiran across patients with ALT <= 3xULN and ALT > 3xULN. For Givosiran, N=53 for ALT <= 3xULN and N=4 for ALT > 3xULN. For AS(N-1)3' Givosiran, N=38 for ALT <= 3xULN and N=4 for ALT > 3xULN. The plots show that the distribution of Cmax is similar between the two ALT groups for both compounds, suggesting a lack of relationship between ALT elevations and Cmax.
L1068: Figure 31 is a scatter plot showing the relationship between ALT elevations and predicted Givosiran Liver Cmax during the first 3 months of Study 002/003 for patients receiving Givosiran 2.5 mg/kg once monthly. The Y-axis represents Predicted Givosiran Liver Cmax (\mu\text{g/g}), ranging from 26 to 38. The X-axis represents ALT status, divided into two groups: ALT \le 3\times\text{ULN} (N=50) and ALT > 3\times\text{ULN} (N=7). The data points for both groups are tightly clustered around a Cmax value of approximately 34 \mu\text{g/g}, indicating no significant difference in predicted Cmax based on ALT elevation status.
L1069: Figure 32 is a scatter plot showing the correlation between ALT elevations and model-predicted Givosiran Liver Cmax during the first 3 months of Study 002/003 for patients receiving Givosiran by different dose regimens. The Y-axis represents Predicted Givosiran Liver Cmax (\mu\text{g/g}), ranging from 30 to 70. The X-axis represents ALT status, divided into two groups: ALT \le 3\times\text{ULN} (N=57) and ALT > 3\times\text{ULN} (N=7). The data points are categorized by dose regimen: 2.5 mg/kg Monthly (red circles), 5 mg/kg Monthly (blue triangles), and 5 mg/kg Quarterly (green squares). The plot shows that predicted Cmax values are generally low (around 30-35 \mu\text{g/g}) for the 2.5 mg/kg Monthly and 5 mg/kg Quarterly regimens, regardless of ALT status. For the 5 mg/kg Monthly regimen, predicted Cmax values are significantly higher (around 50-55 \mu\text{g/g}) for patients with ALT \le 3\times\text{ULN}, but drop sharply to around 30-35 \mu\text{g/g} for patients with ALT > 3\times\text{ULN}.
